OKTO MULTIWAVE SYNTH · USER MANUAL A

OSCILLATORS A1 · A2Wavelet Subtractive

The oscillators A1 and A2 are subtractive, with a wavelet twist. Each starts from a bright waveform — saw, square, pulse, triangle, sine or a noise cycle — and shapes it with a filter that cuts scales instead of frequencies: a wavelet transform breaks every cycle into 11 octave bands of small building blocks, the atoms; the SCALE FILTER turns the bands up and down, SPARSITY keeps only the most important atoms, the POSITION window confines the filter to one part of the cycle, and SOURCE PHASE moves the wave under the atoms. Okto MultiWave Synth has two subtractive oscillators and one additive one and mixes them: this manual is about the subtractive pair; Manual B describes the additive oscillator.

For the web app and the VST3 / VST2 plug-in for WindowsVersion beta 0.3.2 · 2026-10-20Oscillator B1 — read Manual B

1Quick start

From closed to sounding in a few steps — pick the column for the version you use.

Web app BROWSER

  1. Double-click web/index.html to open it in Chrome or Edge (Firefox also works). No installation and no web server are needed — or open the page of the project: pierrearnaudguyot.github.io/Okto-MultiWave-Synth.
  2. Click Play the synth in the welcome window (or Run the demo for a tour of the presets): browsers only allow sound after a click. The status bar turns green: Audio on.
  3. The app opens on A · Warm Pad. Play with the mouse on the keyboard, the digit keys 1 … 0, the letter row A S D F …, or a MIDI keyboard.
  4. Click the folder tabs OSC1 A1 OSC2 A2 OSC3 B1 under the header to see each oscillator's cards. The tabs only choose the panel shown: what you hear is set by the three levels in the OSC MIX card (a lit dot on a folder tab says the oscillator is heard).
  5. Hold a note, show the A1 panel and slowly turn CUTOFF of the SCALE FILTER down. In the SCALOGRAM the rows switch off from the top as bands are removed. Then step the wavelet with ◀ ▶ and sweep again: same filter, different atoms.

Ableton Live VST3 / VST2

  1. Download the plug-in zip from its release page (the web app's DOWNLOAD VST button opens it) and unzip it. Copy the whole OktoMultiWaveSynth.vst3 folder into your VST3 folder (C:\Program Files\Common Files\VST3; vst\install.bat does it and asks for administrator rights) or into your VST2 plug-in folder.
  2. In Live open Preferences → Plug-ins, switch on Use VST3 Plug-in System Folders (or Use VST2 Plug-in Custom Folder) and click Rescan.
  3. In the browser open Plug-ins → OktoPierre → Okto MultiWave Synth and drag it onto a MIDI track. A new instance starts on A · Warm Pad.
  4. Arm the track and play a MIDI keyboard, or press M to use Live's Computer MIDI Keyboard. The panel is the same as in the web app.
TIP

Want to hear what it can do first? ▶ DEMO (web app) plays Beethoven's Appassionata while the presets change every few seconds; EVALUATE SOUNDS (web app and plug-in) lets you hear each of the 1160 new presets and rate them — see Evaluate sounds.

2The panel

The panel is the same in the web app and the plug-in, apart from a few buttons (the web app's DEMO and DOWNLOAD VST, the plug-in's size button, SAVE PRESET and the SYNC buttons of the tempo sync). From top to bottom: the header; the strip of three folder tabs with the plate under it that holds the cards of the oscillator shown (WAVELET, SCALE FILTER, POSITION, TUNE · OWN ENVELOPE · PITCH ENVELOPE) and, at the top right and common to every oscillator, OSC MIX and the SCOPE of the whole sound; the voice FILTER, the AMP ENVELOPE and the MOD ENVELOPE; LFO 1, LFO 2/3 and VOICE; EFFECTS and the PLAY card (CHORD MODE, BASS, TUNING); the keyboard row. With CHORD MODE on, the chord-harp card appears below and the window grows. The credit line “By OktoPierre · beta 0.3.2 · GPL v3” sits under the wheels.

  1. 1Folder tabs — OSC1 A1 · OSC2 A2 · OSC3 B1: which oscillator's cards are shown on the plate; a lit dot = it is heard (the sound is set by OSC MIX).
  2. 2Preset browser — ◀ ▶ step through the presets; click the name for the list.
  3. 3EVALUATE SOUNDS — the listening test of the new presets.
  4. 4DOWNLOAD VST / SAVE PRESET — the web app links to the plug-in; the plug-in has SAVE PRESET here.
  5. 5MANUAL — this manual on an A tab, the other one on a B tab.
  6. 6VOLUME and meter — master level and stereo peak meter.
  7. 7WAVE — the source waveform of the oscillator.
  8. 8ATOM SHAPE — one atom of the selected wavelet.
  9. 9WAVELET · WAVELET 2 — the atom shape; the second one is for WAVELET MORPH.
  10. 10SOURCE CYCLE — the wave before (grey) and after sparsity; “K / nz atoms kept”.
  11. 11PHASE — SOURCE PHASE: moves the wave inside the cycle before the analysis.
  12. 12WIDTH · SPARSITY — pulse width / triangle skew; how many atoms are kept.
  13. 13OSC MIX — three levels, one per oscillator; below them the SUB OSC.
  14. 14SCOPE · SCALOGRAM — one cycle of the whole sound (every oscillator that plays) and its atoms in the 11 bands.
  15. 15SCALE FILTER — a gain for each wavelet band: type, cutoff, slope, resonance, key tracking, envelope, velocity.
  16. 16POSITION — confines the filter or the detail to part of the cycle.
  17. 17TUNE · OWN ENVELOPE · PITCH ENVELOPE — the oscillator's own pitch, its own loudness envelope and its pitch envelope.
  18. 18FILTER — a 24 dB resonant low-pass in every voice (LADDER or OTA), with its own envelope; open by default.
  19. 19AMP ENVELOPE — loudness of the whole sound.
  20. 20MOD ENVELOPE — moves the cutoff of every oscillator by its ENV AMT.
  21. 21LFO 1 — pitch, cutoff, position, wavelet morph, sparsity, resonance and envelope-to-wavelet.
  22. 22LFO 2 / LFO 3 — each moves one control chosen by TARGET: any scale-filter, wavelet or FILTER control, in every oscillator.
  23. 23VOICE — portamento, octave, tuning, polyphony, glide, unison.
  24. 24EFFECTS — saturation, chorus, ping-pong delay and reverb.
  25. 25CHORD MODE · BASS — shows the chord-harp card and turns the keyboard into chords; BASS adds the low note.
  26. 26TUNING — the three-position switch of the oriental keyboard: Western, Arabic, Turkish.
  27. 27Wheels · keyboard — pitch bend, modulation; five octaves.

The header

ControlWhat it does
▶ DEMO ▾ (web app)Plays the default demo, a tour of the presets; the ▾ opens other pieces and "Load a MIDI file…".
Folder tabsThe strip of three tabs under the header — OSC1 A1 · SUBTRACTIVE and OSC2 A2 · SUBTRACTIVE (yellow), OSC3 B1 · ADDITIVE (blue) — chooses the oscillator whose cards are shown on the plate below it; a lit dot says the oscillator is heard. It does not change the sound, and does not mark a preset as modified.
Preset boxThe name of the sound; a dot after it means it was modified since it was loaded. ◀ ▶ step; a click opens the preset browser.
EVALUATE SOUNDSThe listening test of the new presets (see Evaluate sounds); it lights up and says STOP while a session runs.
DOWNLOAD VST · SAVE PRESETThe web app's button opens the plug-in's release page; in the plug-in the same place holds SAVE PRESET.
MANUALOpens the manual of the oscillator shown — Manual A on A1 or A2, Manual B on B1; each links to the other.
VOLUME · meterMaster level (−36 … +6 dB) and the stereo peak meter.

Working with the controls

GestureEffect
Drag up / downChanges the value; a full sweep takes about 180 pixels (120 on a drawbar).
Shift + dragFive times finer.
Mouse wheelSteps of 1 % of the range (0.2 % with Shift); one step on stepped controls.
Double-click, or Ctrl + clickResets the control to its default.
HoverThe label under a knob shows its value with its unit.
Right-click (web app)MIDI learn: then move a knob or fader on your controller (Esc cancels, Delete unlinks).
Greyed-out controlHas no effect with the current settings (WIDTH unless the wave is PULSE or TRI, SEED unless the pattern is RANDOM, the sub oscillator's OCT and SHAPE while its LEVEL is 0).

3Three oscillators

Okto MultiWave Synth plays up to three oscillators in every voice: A1 and A2 (wavelet subtractive) and B1 (wavelet additive). A new patch plays A1 alone; the three levels in the OSC MIX card decide which oscillators sound and how loud. Each oscillator is a small instrument of its own — its tone, its SCALE FILTER, its POSITION window, its tuning, its own envelope and its pitch envelope — while the AMP ENVELOPE, MOD ENVELOPE, LFOs, VOICE settings, effects and sub oscillator are shared by the whole voice.

  • One panel at a time. The folder tabs OSC1 A1 OSC2 A2 OSC3 B1 under the header choose the oscillator: they show one oscillator's cards on the plate (yellow for A1 and A2, blue for B1) while the other two keep their settings and keep sounding. A lit dot on a folder tab says its oscillator is heard (its level is above 0). Switching a tab never changes the sound and never marks a preset as modified. OSC MIX and the SCOPE are common to every oscillator, so they stay in place at the top right whatever tab is shown; the scope and the scalogram draw one cycle of the whole sound (every oscillator that plays, and the sub oscillator, at C4).
  • Levels cost nothing at 0. An oscillator whose level is 0 is not computed and its wave tables are not even built, so presets that use two oscillators cost about twice one — not three times. A level is smoothed over 20 ms, so it can be moved while playing.
  • Every preset uses at least two oscillators (the 22 TOP Sounds keep their one or two of the Wavelet Synthesizer). The letters in the names of the new presets — A1A2, A1B1, A1A2B1 — say which.

OSC MIX and SUB OSC

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
A1host: A1 Level0 – 100 %default 100%The level of oscillator A1 (subtractive). 100 % on a new patch.The tone card of each oscillator has the same knob.
A2host: A2 Level0 – 100 %default 0%The level of A2 (subtractive).A second subtractive voice: a detuned copy, an octave, a different wave or filter.
B1host: B1 Level0 – 100 %default 0%The level of B1 (additive).Often the body, the choir or the shimmer on top of the subtractive oscillators.
LEVELhost: Sub Level0 – 100 %default 0%The sub oscillator: a sine, triangle or square one or two octaves below the note, unfiltered, once per voice.Adds weight; it does not go through the scale filters.
OCThost: Sub Octave−1 · −2default −1 octave−1 or −2 octaves.
SHAPEhost: Sub ShapeSIN · TRI · SQRdefault SineSine, triangle or square.

TUNE, OWN ENVELOPE and PITCH ENVELOPE

The wide card at the bottom of the plate belongs to the oscillator shown (the folder tab says which). It is what turns three oscillators into a sound with a front and a body.

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
OCTAVEhost: A1 Octave−3 … +3default 0Transposes the oscillator by whole octaves.A2 an octave down under A1 is the classic "saw + sub saw".
SEMIhost: A1 Semitones−12 … +12 semitonesdefault 0 stTransposes by semitones.+7 gives a fifth; +12 an octave.
FINEhost: A1 Fine Tune−100 … +100 centsdefault 0 ctFine tuning in cents.±6–15 cents between two oscillators gives a beating, chorused sound.
OWN ENVhost: A1 Own EnvelopeOFF · ONdefault OffSwitches the oscillator's own envelope on. The oscillator is then multiplied by its own ADSR as well as the AMP ENVELOPE, so it can only be shorter than the voice: a short decay makes it a percussive attack (a click, a pluck, a breath); a slow attack makes it fade in under the others.The ADSR display next to it draws the shape.
ATTACKhost: A1 Env Attack1 ms – 10 sdefault 1.0 msAttack of the own envelope (linear).
DECAYhost: A1 Env Decay1 ms – 10 sdefault 250 msDecay to the sustain level (exponential; the time to fall by 60 dB).20–80 ms for a click, 200–600 ms for a pluck.
SUSTAINhost: A1 Env Sustain0 – 100 %default 0%Sustain level.0 % makes a one-shot attack that dies by itself.
RELEASEhost: A1 Env Release1 ms – 10 sdefault 150 msRelease after the key is released.
PITCH ENVhost: A1 Pitch Env Amount−48 … +48 semitonesdefault 0 stThe pitch envelope: the oscillator starts this many semitones away from the note and glides to it (negative = it starts below).+12 … +30 with a short TIME is a kick or a zap; a small value is a "blip" on every note.
TIMEhost: A1 Pitch Env Time5 ms – 2 sdefault 80 msHow long the glide takes to come back to the note: exponential, the time to fall by 60 dB (half of the distance is covered in about a tenth of it).

The table shows the ranges and defaults of A1's controls; A2 and B1 have the same ones. The own envelope has no effect while its switch is off.

Ideas, and presets to study

IdeaHowLook at
A percussive attackOne oscillator with its own envelope: a noise cycle played low (OCTAVE −2 or −3) through a high-pass at band 6–9, DECAY 20–40 ms, SUSTAIN 0; the other oscillator is the body.002 A1A2 · Pick Bass, 190 A1A2 · Snare, 193 A1A2 · Clap
A pitch dropA sine with PITCH ENV +12 … +30 semitones and a TIME of 30–60 ms.189 A1A2 · Kick, 004 A1A2 · Sub Drop, 198 A1B1 · Tom
A fat pairTwo oscillators of the same kind a few cents apart, or an octave apart with different cutoffs.005 A1A2 · Reese Duo, 001 A1A2 · Deep Saw Bass, 075 A1A2 · Velvet Pad
Additive body, subtractive attackB1 for the body (drawbars, atoms), A1 as a short, bright start.058 A1B1 · Rhodes Warm, 062 A1B1 · Dream Keys, 120 A1B1 · Solo Violin
Two LFOs on the waveletLFO 2 on one wavelet control (the SPARSITY, the window position, the SOURCE PHASE …), LFO 3 on another, at unrelated speeds; then set their amounts to 0 one at a time to hear what each does.501 A1A2 · Abyss Sweep Signal, 502 A1B1 · Resonance Storm, 504 A1B1 · Taper & Vibes
A real filter on topThe voice FILTER with an envelope: a low cutoff, some RESO and a positive ENV AMT with a short decay is the classic synth bass or pluck; aim LFO 3 at FILTER CUTOFF for a wah.554 A1A2 · Hollow Bass, 559 A1B1 · Amber Morph, 572 A1A2B1 · Stair & Tap

4The subtractive oscillators A1 · A2

An A oscillator creates its raw material from a source wave: draw the cycle, analyse it, optionally thin out the atoms, split it into 11 band layers (plus a "dry" layer: their sum, the unfiltered wave). Every voice then plays these layers with gains set by the SCALE FILTER.

Every oscillator plays one cycle of 2048 samples, analysed by a periodic wavelet transform into 11 bands: band b has 2b−1 atoms and covers about one octave of harmonics (band 1 the fundamental, band 2 the 2nd harmonic, band 3 harmonics 3–4, band 4 harmonics 5–8 …). The analysis is pitch-synchronous, so the bands follow the note. To keep high notes alias-free each band is stored in octave-wise band-limited versions: the finest bands only exist on low notes — at middle C only bands 1–7 are played, at C2 bands 1–9. The SCALOGRAM draws the atoms of the 11 bands of the oscillator shown (B1 at the bottom).

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
WAVEhost: Source WaveSAW · SQUARE · PULSE · TRI · SINE · NOISEdefault SawThe waveform that is analysed: one cycle, its average removed and its peak normalised.SAW and NOISE have detail in every band: the most to filter.
WIDTHhost: Source Width2 – 98 %default 50%Only for PULSE and TRI (greyed out otherwise). PULSE: the part of the cycle spent high. TRI: where the peak sits — 50 % is symmetric.With Haar, 25 %, 50 % and 75 % leave almost nothing to filter.
SPARSITYhost: Sparsity0 – 100 %default 0%Keeps only the largest atoms of the cycle and silences the rest: 0 % keeps every atom the wave needs, 50 % about the square root of that number, 100 % a single atom. The SOURCE CYCLE display reads “K / nz ATOMS KEPT”.A lossy-codec timbre: glassy, slightly digital tones. Each change rebuilds the tables (crossfaded).
PHASEhost: Source Phase0 – 360°default 0°SOURCE PHASE: moves the wave inside the cycle before the analysis. The wavelet transform is not shift-invariant, so the same wave at another phase is made of other atoms and the filter, SPARSITY and the window act on it differently.Strong with Haar, Daubechies 2 and 4; weak with Daubechies 20. LFO 2 can move it (TARGET PHASE).
WAVELEThost: WaveletHaar · DB2 · DB4 · DB8 · Sym8 · Coif3 · DB20default Daubechies 4The shape of the atoms.Changing it while holding a note crossfades.
WaveOne cycleCharacter
SAWRamps down across the cycle, jumps back up at the start.All harmonics; fine atoms only at the jump. The classic subtractive source.
SQUAREHigh for the first half, low for the second.Odd harmonics, hollow. With Haar it is a single atom: nothing to filter.
PULSEHigh for WIDTH of the cycle.Thinner and more nasal away from 50 %.
TRIRises to the peak at WIDTH × 360°, then falls.Soft, flute-like.
SINEOne sine cycle.Little to filter; a pure reference, or a sub body.
NOISEOne fixed random cycle.Played at the note's pitch it is a buzzy, pitched timbre, not hiss. A noise oscillator is band-limited at the note: for dense, loud noise (hats, clicks) play it low (OCTAVE −2 or −3) and use the high bands.
A BEAUTIFUL CONSEQUENCE

The square wave that will not filter

With the Haar wavelet the band-1 atom is one cycle of a square wave, so a SQUARE analysed with Haar is exactly one atom: the low-pass has nothing to remove. For progressive sweeps on a square choose a smooth wavelet (Daubechies 8, Symlet 8, Daubechies 20), or use the effect on purpose.

Sparsity

Keeping only the K largest atoms is what wavelet lossy codecs do to save bits; here it is a timbre control. The range is relative to the wave: a SAW analysed with Daubechies 8 has only 96 non-zero atoms (a straight ramp needs no fine atoms, only the jump does). SPARSITY keeps K = nz1 − SPARSITY of them, so the knob does something over its whole range for every wave; sources that need few atoms change in clear jumps.

The wavelet chooses the atom

Each oscillator has its own WAVELET (the step control under the atom picture): seven classic wavelets from the 2-tap Haar step to the 40-tap Daubechies 20. Short wavelets are precise in time — sharp edges, crisp POSITION window borders — but their bands overlap; long wavelets separate the octaves cleanly. For sweeps that sound like a conventional filter choose Daubechies 8 or longer; for stepped, digital colours Haar or Daubechies 2.

WaveletTapsCharacter
Haar2A square step up and down: sharp, digital, 8-bit; every removed band halves the number of steps of the wave.
Daubechies 24Short and lopsided: asymmetric ringing, gritty and slightly metallic.
Daubechies 48A good all-rounder with mild, asymmetric ringing.
Daubechies 816Longer and smoother; filter sweeps start to sound analogue.
Symlet 816Almost symmetric: cleaner, more balanced edges than Daubechies 8.
Coiflet 318Nearly symmetric and very smooth for its length.
Daubechies 2040Long and almost sine-like: each band is close to a pure octave of harmonics — the smoothest, most "Fourier-like" filtering.

WAVELET MORPH builds every oscillator with a second wavelet too (WAVELET 2) and moves between the two with MORPH, LFO 1 (its WAVELET knob) and the mod envelope (ENV>WAV); it acts on each oscillator that plays and is heard where the wavelet shapes the sound.

5Scale filter and position window

The SCALE FILTER does not cut frequencies: it gives each of the 11 bands its own gain and the sound is the sum of the band layers. A low-pass removes the fine atoms and keeps the coarse ones. There is no phase shift and no feedback, and the character of a sweep depends on the wavelet: with Haar every removed band halves the number of steps of the wave, with Daubechies 20 the bands are nearly pure octaves. Every oscillator has its own filter and window; the tabs show the cards of the oscillator selected. The table lists the controls of A1; the other oscillators of the same kind have the same ones.

SCALE FILTER

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
TYPEhost: Filter TypeLOW-PASS · HIGH-PASS · BAND-PASS · BAND-REJdefault Low-passShape of the gain curve over the bands: LOW-PASS keeps the coarse bands, HIGH-PASS the fine ones, BAND-PASS about one band around the cutoff, BAND-REJ removes the band at the cutoff.Band-pass on a low cutoff gives thin, nasal tones.
CUTOFFhost: Cutoff0 – 12default band 12.0Where the filter acts, in bands: 1 = the fundamental, 11 = the finest atoms, 12 = beyond them (a low-pass is fully open).At middle C only bands 1–7 exist: cutoffs 8–12 sound the same there.
SLOPEhost: Slope6 – 96 dB/banddefault +24.0 dB/bandHow steeply the bands beyond the cutoff are attenuated; one band ≈ one octave, so 24 dB/band behaves like a 24 dB/octave filter. For BAND-REJ it sets the notch width.Steep slopes act like faders that switch bands on and off.
RESOhost: Resonance0 – 24 dBdefault 0.0 dBBoosts the bands around the cutoff by up to 24 dB. A band boost, not feedback: it never self-oscillates.Resonance adds level: watch the meter.
KEY TRKhost: Key Tracking0 – 100 %default 100%100 %: the cutoff follows the note. 0 %: it stays at a fixed frequency (C4 = 261.63 Hz).50 % is the familiar "darker as you go up".
ENV AMThost: Envelope Amount−11 … +11 bandsdefault 0.0 bandsHow far the MOD ENVELOPE moves this oscillator's cutoff: −11 … +11 bands at its peak.Each oscillator has its own amount: the same envelope can open one and close another.
VEL AMThost: Velocity Amount−6 … +6 bandsdefault 0.0 bandsVelocity → cutoff: the shift is amount × (velocity − 1).+2 … +3 bands: soft notes darker.

The cutoff actually used by a voice, recomputed every 32 samples: cutoff = CUTOFF + ENV AMT × mod envelope + LFO 1 CUTOFF × LFO 1 (+ LFO 2 and LFO 3 aimed at CUTOFF) + VEL AMT × (velocity − 1) + (1 − KEY TRK) × log₂(261.63 Hz ÷ note frequency). (The voice FILTER of the next chapters is another filter, on frequencies: do not mix them up.)

POSITION window

Wavelet atoms live at definite places in the cycle, so an A oscillator can filter one part of the cycle and leave the rest untouched: soften only the falling edge of a square, or darken only the reset of a saw.

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
POSITIONhost: Position0° – 360°default 0°Centre of the window inside the cycle, 0° … 360°.180° is the falling edge of SQUARE.
WIDTHhost: Window Width5 – 100 %default 50%How much of the cycle the window covers.Narrow windows are automatically widened on high notes (no aliasing).
DEPTHhost: Window Depth0 – 100 %default 0%How strongly the window confines the filter: 0 % = off (the filter acts everywhere); 100 % = the filter acts only inside the window, outside it the dry cycle plays.LFO 1 and LFO 2 can move the window.

6Envelopes

Two ADSR envelopes run for every voice, plus the own envelope and pitch envelope of each oscillator (see TUNE, OWN ENVELOPE and PITCH ENVELOPE). Attacks are linear; decays and releases are exponential (the time given is the time to fall by 60 dB).

AMP ENVELOPE

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
ATTACKhost: Amp Attack1 ms – 10 sdefault 3.0 msRise from silence to full level.1–5 ms is click-free for most sounds; 0.3–1 s for pads.
DECAYhost: Amp Decay1 ms – 10 sdefault 400 msFall from full level to the sustain level.For plucks set SUSTAIN 0 % and use DECAY as the note length.
SUSTAINhost: Amp Sustain0 – 100 %default 80%Level held while the key (or the sustain pedal) is down.
RELEASEhost: Amp Release1 ms – 10 sdefault 300 msFade after the key is released. The voice is freed at −100 dB.Long releases overlap: raise VOICES for pads.
VELOCITYhost: Amp Velocity0 – 100 %default 50%How much velocity sets the loudness. 0 % = every note equally loud.The computer keyboard plays at velocity 80 %.

MOD ENVELOPE

It moves the cutoff of every oscillator by that oscillator's ENV AMT (so with ENV AMT at 0 it does nothing there), and the WAVELET MORPH by ENV>WAV. It ignores velocity — use VEL AMT for that.

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
ATTACKhost: Mod Attack1 ms – 10 sdefault 3.0 msAttack of the modulation envelope.A slow mod attack gives brassy swells.
DECAYhost: Mod Decay1 ms – 10 sdefault 500 msDecay to the mod sustain level.
SUSTAINhost: Mod Sustain0 – 100 %default 30%Mod level held while the key is down.
RELEASEhost: Mod Release1 ms – 10 sdefault 500 msMod release.

7LFO 1, LFO 2 and LFO 3

Three LFOs: LFO 1, and LFO 2 and LFO 3, which share a card (the small 2 | 3 switch next to its title shows one or the other). They are shared by all voices and oscillators and run freely (not restarted by new notes): in a chord every voice moves together. For a movement that starts with each note use the MOD ENVELOPE.

LFO 1

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
SHAPEhost: LFO 1 ShapeSINE · TRI · SAW · SQR · S&Hdefault SineSINE, TRI, SAW, SQR or S&H (sample & hold: a new random value per cycle).
RATEhost: LFO 1 Rate0.05 – 20 Hzdefault 4.0 HzSpeed, one cycle per 20 s to 20 per second. In the plug-in, with SYNC on, a note value at the host's tempo.5–6 Hz for vibrato.
PITCHhost: LFO 1 to Pitch0 – 100 centsdefault 0 ctVibrato depth in cents (the mod wheel adds up to 50 more).
CUTOFFhost: LFO 1 to Cutoff0 – ±6 bandsdefault ±0.0 bandsMoves the cutoff of every oscillator by up to ± this many bands.
POSITIONhost: LFO 1 to Position0 – ±180°default ±0°Moves the POSITION window of every oscillator by up to ± this many degrees.Only audible where the window's DEPTH is above 0 %.
WAVELEThost: LFO 1 to Wavelet Morph0 – 100 %default 0%Moves the WAVELET MORPH.Needs a WAVELET 2 different from the oscillator's WAVELET.
SPARSITYhost: LFO 1 to Sparsity0 – 100 %default 0%Moves the SPARSITY of every subtractive oscillator by up to ± this share of the range: the atoms that are kept come and go, and the timbre swings between a rich tone and a few atoms.Starts to show where the wavelet is not Haar (a Daubechies 8 saw has only 96 atoms). The tables are built at nine sparsities while this is above 0 %.
RESOhost: LFO 1 to Resonance0 – 24 dBdefault 0.0 dBMoves the RESO of every scale filter by up to ± this many dB (kept between 0 and 24 dB): the resonant peak of the filter pulses.Needs a cutoff inside the sound (bands 3–8) and a little resonance to start from.
ENV>WAVhost: LFO 1 to Envelope>Wavelet0 – 100 %default 0%Makes the reach of the MOD ENVELOPE on the WAVELET MORPH (ENV>WAV) swing: ENV>WAV + this amount × LFO 1.Needs a WAVELET 2 different from the oscillator's WAVELET, and an ENV>WAV above 0 or a MORPH to move.

LFO 2 and LFO 3

LFO 2 and LFO 3 are the same LFO twice: each moves one control, in every oscillator at once, by the same amount: the one chosen in its TARGET menu — CUTOFF, SLOPE, RESO, KEY TRK, ENV AMT or VEL AMT of the scale filters, POSITION, WIDTH or DEPTH of the windows, the PHASE, SPARSITY or SRC WIDTH of the subtractive oscillators, the TILT, ODD BARS, EVEN BARS or SCALE SUS of the additive ones, the MORPH between two wavelets, or the FILTER CUTOFF or FILTER RESONANCE of the voice FILTER: every control of the wavelet oscillators, of the scale filter and of the voice filter. Each oscillator keeps its own base value; the LFO moves them in step. The two LFOs can aim at different controls (two independent movements) or at the same one (their moves add up). A target acts on every oscillator that has the control, and cannot be limited to one: aim it at a control that only one kind has (SPARSITY, PHASE or SRC WIDTH for the subtractive oscillators; TILT, ODD BARS, EVEN BARS or SCALE SUS for the additive one) to move that kind only.

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
TARGEThost: LFO 2 TargetCUTOFF · SLOPE · RESO · KEY TRK · ENV AMT · VEL AMT · POSITION · WIDTH · DEPTH · PHASE · SPARSITY · SRC WIDTH · TILT · ODD BARS · EVEN BARS · SCALE SUS · MORPH · FLT CUT · FLT RESOdefault CutoffWhat LFO 2 moves (the menu next to its title; the mouse wheel steps through it). LFO 3 has the same menu.LFO 1 already has CUTOFF and POSITION: give LFO 2 another target for two independent movements.
SHAPEhost: LFO 2 ShapeSINE · TRI · SAW · SQR · S&Hdefault SineThe same five shapes as LFO 1.
RATEhost: LFO 2 Rate0.05 – 20 Hzdefault 1.0 HzSpeed, independent of LFO 1; note values with SYNC in the plug-in.A rate that does not divide LFO 1's makes the two drift against each other.
AMOUNThost: LFO 2 Amount−100 … +100 %default 0%How far the target moves: up to ± this share of half its range, kept in range (FILTER CUTOFF: half of its ten octaves, so 30 % is about ± 1.5 octaves). 0 % (the default) switches LFO 2 off.Start around 20–30 %.
TARGEThost: LFO 3 TargetCUTOFF · SLOPE · RESO · KEY TRK · ENV AMT · VEL AMT · POSITION · WIDTH · DEPTH · PHASE · SPARSITY · SRC WIDTH · TILT · ODD BARS · EVEN BARS · SCALE SUS · MORPH · FLT CUT · FLT RESOdefault CutoffWhat LFO 3 moves: the same menu as LFO 2. When both aim at the same control their moves add.Use LFO 3 for a second control of the wavelet oscillators: LFO 2 on SPARSITY, LFO 3 on the WINDOW position, for instance.
SHAPEhost: LFO 3 ShapeSINE · TRI · SAW · SQR · S&Hdefault SineThe same five shapes.
RATEhost: LFO 3 Rate0.05 – 20 Hzdefault 1.0 HzSpeed, independent of the two other LFOs; note values with SYNC in the plug-in.Rates that do not divide each other make the LFOs drift apart.
AMOUNThost: LFO 3 Amount−100 … +100 %default 0%How far the target moves, as for LFO 2. 0 % (the default) switches LFO 3 off.

PHASE, SPARSITY and SRC WIDTH (SOURCE WIDTH: the pulse width of a PULSE source or the skew of a TRI one) change what the wavelet transform sees, so the subtractive oscillators' tables are built at several values — 12 phases round the cycle, 9 sparsities from 0 to 100 %, 9 widths from 2 to 98 % (about 1 MB per set) — and each voice crossfades between the two nearest: a smooth scan through the timbres. At AMOUNT 0 nothing extra is built. One kind of scan per oscillator at a time: SPARSITY (from LFO 1, LFO 2 or LFO 3) comes first, then PHASE, then SRC WIDTH. TILT, ODD BARS, EVEN BARS and SCALE SUS move the additive oscillators' drawbars (odd = bands 1, 3, 5 …; even = bands 2, 4, 6 …) and the level their bands decay to; MORPH adds to the WAVELET MORPH position. FILTER CUTOFF moves the cutoff of the voice FILTER (in octaves: the whole range is ten octaves) and FILTER RESONANCE its resonance; either one switches the filter on, even while its own settings are open.

SuperModulation

The idea of the instrument is that every timbre control acts on the scales of one cycle of the wave — and so can an LFO, while the note sounds. Moving the cutoff of a scale filter, the resonance, the SPARSITY, the window, the SOURCE PHASE or the morph between two wavelets does not sweep a Fourier filter: the harmonics are re-coupled according to the wavelet, so the timbre swims in a way an ordinary filter sweep does not. The SuperModulation section of the presets (the first one in the lists, 412 sounds) is made of such sounds: two oscillators of very different character, and LFO 1, LFO 2 and LFO 3 each moving one to three of these controls at unrelated speeds. In every preset of beta 0.3, LFO 2 and / or LFO 3 move wavelet controls — mostly of the subtractive oscillators — and the SuperModulation ones use both. Open one, then set the amounts of the LFOs to 0 one by one to hear what each does.

Tempo sync (plug-in only)

In the plug-in a SYNC button sits at the right of the LFO 1 title, on the LFO 2/3 card (one for each of the two LFOs) and at the delay heading. Lit, the RATE (or the delay's TIME) becomes a note value at the host's tempo (120 BPM when the host gives none) and follows its tempo changes. The note values run from 4/1 to 1/32 for the LFOs and 1/32 to 1/1 for the delay (T = triplet, D = dotted). SYNC sets the speed only; the LFO is not restarted on the beat. SYNC is off by default, and the web app has none (a browser page has no tempo).

8Voice, filter and effects

VOICE

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
PORTAMENTOhost: PortamentoOFF · POLY · LEGATOdefault POLYWhen notes glide; GLIDE sets how long (0 = nothing glides). POLY: every voice glides from its own previous note, so chord changes glide voice by voice. LEGATO: only while another key is held.LEGATO for expressive leads, POLY for gliding chords.
OCTAVEhost: Octave−3 … +3default 0Transposes the whole instrument by octaves (each oscillator also has its own TUNE).
FINEhost: Fine Tune−100 … +100 centsdefault 0 ctFine tuning in cents.
VOICEShost: Voices1 – 16default 8Notes sounding at once; 1 = monophonic legato with last-note priority.Pads need 8 or more.
GLIDEhost: Glideoff – 2 sdefault offPortamento time.30–80 ms for leads.
UNISONhost: Unison1 – 4default 1Stacked copies of every note (1–4).Cost = voices × unison × oscillators.
DETUNEhost: Unison Detune0 – 50 centsdefault +12 ctSpread of the unison copies in cents.8–15 cents for warmth.
STEREOhost: Stereo Width0 – 100 %default 70%Spreads the unison copies across the stereo field.No effect with UNISON 1.

When all voices are busy a new note takes the oldest released voice first, then the oldest held one. Each note plays at about −8 dB to leave headroom for chords. A preset can switch mono ↔ poly; a note off always finds its voice.

FILTER

The FILTER card (under the plate, left of the AMP ENVELOPE) is a conventional filter in every voice: a four-pole (24 dB per octave) resonant low-pass that works on the voice after its oscillators are mixed and before its AMP ENVELOPE. It cuts frequencies, on top of the SCALE FILTERs, which cut scales. Two characters, chosen with the LADDER | OTA switch: LADDER saturates at every stage — dark and gritty, and the resonance thins the low end as it rises; OTA saturates only the input of its feedback loop and keeps part of the low end — rounder and smoother. The cutoff a voice uses is CUTOFF × 2ENV AMT × 8 × FILTER ENVELOPE + KEY TRK × (note − C4) ÷ 12 + LFO 2 + LFO 3 (the exponent is in octaves). It is open by default (CUTOFF at its maximum, no resonance, no drive, no envelope): then it is not even computed, and every sound made before it exists is unchanged. Turning any of its controls, or aiming an LFO at it, switches it on.

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
TYPEhost: Filter TypeLADDER · OTAdefault Ladder 24 dBLADDER: every stage saturates, the resonance takes the low end away; OTA: only the input of the loop saturates, the low end is partly kept.LADDER for gritty basses and leads, OTA for rounder pads and keys.
CUTOFFhost: Filter Cutoff20 – 20000 Hzdefault 20000.0 HzThe frequency above which the sound is attenuated (24 dB per octave); exponential scale, 20 Hz to 20 kHz. At the top it is open.Play a note and sweep it; add RESO for the classic wah.
RESOhost: Filter Resonance0 – 100 %default 0%Feeds the output back to the input: a peak at the cutoff. At 100 % the filter is just above the point where it sings by itself.The resonance adds level: watch the meter.
DRIVEhost: Filter Drive0 – 100 %default 0%Saturates the sound before the filter (a tanh, level-compensated): 0 % = clean.With a resonance it makes the filter growl.
KEY TRKhost: Filter Key Tracking0 – 100 %default 0%How much the cutoff follows the note (100 % = one octave of cutoff per octave of pitch, from C4).High notes stay as bright as low notes.
ENV AMThost: Filter Envelope Amount−100 … +100 %default 0%How far the FILTER ENVELOPE (the ADSR next to it) moves the cutoff: up to ± 8 octaves. Negative values close the filter as the envelope rises.Combine a moderate cutoff with a positive ENV AMT and a short decay for the classic "pluck" of a synth bass.
ATTACKhost: Filter Env Attack1 ms – 10 sdefault 5.0 msAttack of the FILTER ENVELOPE.A slow attack opens a pad.
DECAYhost: Filter Env Decay1 ms – 10 sdefault 300 msDecay to the sustain level.
SUSTAINhost: Filter Env Sustain0 – 100 %default 50%Level the envelope holds while the key is down (0 % = the filter closes again, 100 % = it stays open).
RELEASEhost: Filter Env Release1 ms – 10 sdefault 300 msRelease.

Each voice has its own filter and its own FILTER ENVELOPE, started by its note; the cutoff glides smoothly from block to block, so it does not zipper. LFO 2 and LFO 3 can move the CUTOFF (FILTER CUTOFF) and the RESONANCE (FILTER RESONANCE) of every voice together.

EFFECTS

After the voice FILTER (above), in the order of the sound: saturation → string-machine triple chorus → ping-pong delay → reverb → DC blocker → master volume → soft limiter (never above 0 dBFS).

ControlRange · defaultWhat it doesTip
DRIVEhost: Drive0 – 100 %default 0%Saturation: a smooth tanh soft-clipper driven by up to +24 dB, level-compensated. 0 % = bypassed.20–40 % thickens basses.
CHORUShost: Chorus0 – 100 %default 0%Chorus amount: three delay lines swept by a slow and a fast LFO, 120° apart, spread left / centre / right.Widens pads, strings and organs.
RATEhost: Chorus Rate0.1 – 3 Hzdefault 0.70 HzSpeed of the chorus LFOs.
DEPTHhost: Chorus Depth0 – 100 %default 60%Depth of the sweep.
TIMEhost: Delay Time10 ms – 1 sdefault 350 msDelay time (up to 4 s). Changing it while echoes ring makes them glide in pitch. With SYNC on in the plug-in, a note value.
FEEDBACKhost: Delay Feedback0 – 90 %default 35%Feedback: more repeats. The echoes alternate left and right and get darker.Above 70 % the repeats last for seconds.
DLY MIXhost: Delay Mix0 – 100 %default 0%Level of the echoes.
SIZEhost: Reverb Size0 – 100 %default 60%Reverb decay, from a small room to a long hall.
REV MIXhost: Reverb Mix0 – 100 %default 0%Level of the reverb.15–25 % for most sounds; 40 % + for pads.
VOLUMEhost: Master Volume−36 … +6 dBdefault −3.0 dBMaster output level, the small knob in the header.Lower it when the meter sits at the top.

The smoothing of the delay, its filter, the master level and the reverb damping have the same time constants at 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz. An instance with nothing sounding and no echo left to come does not compute its effects (it costs nothing while idle).

9Chord mode

The CHORD MODE button of the PLAY card (above the keyboard, at the right) turns the keyboard into a chord harp: the panel grows and the CHORD KEYBOARD card appears below it. With CHORD MODE off the card is not shown and the keyboard plays plain notes.

  • One octave chooses the chord type and makes no sound: KEYS OCT (C3–B3 by default, tinted purple on the keyboard). Every key above it plays the whole chord with one finger; keys pressed while the root is held play as themselves; keys below it play a bass line.
  • Twelve pads in the layout of the selector octave — Maj, Min, 7, m7, Maj7, Sus2, Sus4, Dim, Aug, 5, Add9, Note: the white keys carry the major and bright chords, the black keys the minor, altered or bare ones. A click on a pad chooses the type and switches CHORD MODE on. 22 chord types in all (the Chord Type parameter has the others: 6, m6, m7b5, dim7, 9, m9, maj9, 7sus4, mMaj7, 6/9).
  • VOICING: STACKED (root + intervals) or FOLDED (the chord's notes inside the octave of the root, as on an electronic chord harp); STRUM (0–100 ms) starts the notes one after the other; the strum plate strums the chord with the mouse.
  • BASS (the button under CHORD MODE, on by default) adds a bass note with every chord: the root two octaves below, a little louder (+3 dB), never lower than C1. A chord keeps the level of a triad whatever its size (the notes of a seventh with its bass are turned down a little), so chords never press on the limiter.
  • The chord follows the left hand. Hold a note with the right hand and press another chord on the selector octave: the held chord changes at once, even for the note already pressed. Only the notes of the chord now played sound — the notes that are not in the new chord fade out in a few milliseconds, the ones the two chords share go on without being played again. The left hand has a memory: the last chord chosen stays chosen after its key is released and applies to the next right-hand note. Clicking a pad, VOICING or BASS while a note is held changes the held chord the same way.
  • The chord's notes are ordinary notes: sustain, portamento, voice allocation and every sound parameter work as usual, and a chord never uses more voices than VOICES. A note shared by the chord and a held key sounds until both release it.
  • The chord settings are plug-in parameters (automatable, saved with the Live Set) but never part of a preset: loading a sound does not change how you play. A selector key updates the Chord Type parameter.

10Oriental keyboard

Beta 0.2 adds an oriental keyboard with quarter tones, as on the oriental keyboards of Yamaha. The three-position switch TUNING sits in the PLAY card, to the right of CHORD MODE:

  • WEST — equal temperament, as always (the default).
  • ARAB — the Arabic tuning: 24 tones to the octave, the quarter tone being 50 cents. The keys that are half-flat in the scale chosen are lowered by a quarter tone.
  • TURK — the Turkish tuning of the makams: 53 commas to the octave (a comma is 22.6 cents; the values of the Arel-Ezgi-Uzdilek system), so a third or a seventh is a comma or two away from the Western one.

Outside WEST two more controls show in the PLAY card, under the switch: MAQAM chooses the scale (Rast, Bayati / Uşşak, Hijaz / Hicaz, Saba, Nahawand / Buselik) and TONIC the key it starts on (the scale is moved to it: Rast on D is Rast starting at D). The keys the tuning moves carry a small amber arrow on the keyboard (pointing down: lower than the piano key). A key that is not a degree of the scale keeps its piano pitch, so the sharps and flats you need stay in tune.

  • Every note follows the tuning: the keys, the chord harp's notes (a chord on Rast has its half-flat third), the sub oscillator, the notes a MIDI file plays — except the demo and EVALUATE SOUNDS, which always play in equal temperament so that they are not out of tune with the music.
  • TUNING, MAQAM and TONIC are playing settings, like CHORD MODE: they are automatable parameters, saved with the Live Set, and never part of a preset.
  • Examples. Rast on C, Arabic: play C D E F G A B C — E and B are a quarter tone low, the rest is as on the piano. Bayati on D: D then E, a quarter tone flat, F G A B♭ C. Hicaz on D, Turkish: D E♭ F♯ G A B♭ C with the commas of the Turkish system.

The tunings are the usual approximations of keyboards (half-flat = −50 cents; Turkish values rounded to a comma): they do not replace an oud or a ney, but they make the characteristic intervals playable on a piano keyboard. In the Arabic tuning Rast retunes the third and the seventh, Bayati and Saba the second degree, and Hijaz and Nahawand use the piano's semitones (nothing to retune); the Turkish tuning gives all five their commas.

11Presets

Okto MultiWave Synth has 1182 factory presets: OktoPierre's 22 TOP Sounds (unchanged from the Wavelet Synthesizer beta 0.7: they sound exactly as there, from B · Mirror Sub Bass to A · Laser Sweep) and 1160 new presets: 852 of beta 0.3 (numbers 501–1400: 71 in each of the 12 categories), in which LFO 2 and / or LFO 3 move wavelet controls of the oscillators (mostly of the subtractive ones) and about two in three also use the new voice FILTER; 175 of beta 0.2 (numbers 201–500), in which LFO 1 and LFO 2 move the controls of the wavelet oscillators and A1 and A2 are very different from each other; and 133 of beta 0.1 (001–200, unchanged), sorted into the categories SuperModulation, Bass, Lead, Keys, Organ, Pad, Strings, Brass & Wind, Pluck, Bells & Mallets, FX and Drums & Perc. Beta 0.3 removed the second additive oscillator (B2): the presets that used it left the list, and their numbers are not used again, so the ratings you gave to the others stay valid. A new preset's name is its number, the oscillators it uses (A1A2, A1B1, A1A2B1 …) and a title: 501 A1A2 · Abyss Sweep Signal. Every new preset uses at least two oscillators, often one of them as the attack; their loudness is matched to the TOP Sounds. The SuperModulation category (412 sounds) holds the ones whose life is the LFOs moving the scale filter, the sparsity, the window, the source phase, the morph and the additive scales (see SuperModulation). The preset browser at the right follows the preset box: step with ◀ ▶ or the name's menu and it shows the category of the sound and the sound itself. ◀ ▶ step through them in that order; clicking the name opens the list (the preset browser at the right of the panel: TOP Sounds and the categories, one click plays a sound, the arrow keys ↑ ↓ ← → too).

Saving your own sounds

  • Web app. The buttons at the right of the status bar: Save preset keeps the sound in this browser's storage (the USER PRESETS group), Export downloads it as a .json file, Import loads one. User presets live in that browser profile only: use Export for anything you want to keep.
  • Plug-in. See The plug-in: SAVE PRESET, the user bank, the presets file. You can also keep sounds with Live (a device preset, an Instrument Rack, the Live Set).

1088 A1A2 · Laser Ripple LaserA1 + A2An electronic zap: a sine that dives through the octaves with a hiss on top; each note's envelope pushes the wavelet towards Daubechies 20, by an amount the LFO swings, LFO 3 swells the resonance, LFO 2 sweeps the cutoff of the low-pass filter, and a 24 dB OTA low-pass (2968 Hz) that opens on every note.
1089 A1B1 · Slope TomA1 + B1A tom: a round sine with a falling pitch and mirror atoms for the skin; LFO 3 slides the source inside its cycle, so the atoms meet other parts of the wave (source phase), LFO 2 sharpens and relaxes the edge of the scale filter, and a 24 dB OTA low-pass (5730 Hz) that opens on every note with some bite.
1090 A1A2 · Edge of RattleA1 + A2A snare: a tone that falls a little and a bright burst of noise with a short tail; LFO 2 carries the window of the scale filter round the cycle, LFO 3 sharpens and relaxes the edge of the scale filter, and each note opens its scale filter on its own envelope.
1091 A1A2 · Bite SnapA1 + A2A snare: a tone that falls a little and a bright burst of noise with a short tail; LFO 3 thins and thickens the atoms (sparsity), LFO 2 widens and narrows the filtered window, and a 24 dB OTA low-pass (2091 Hz) that opens on every note.
1092 A1A2 · Orbit DrumA1 + A2A kick: a sine that falls from high to low in a blink, with a click on top; LFO 2 thins and thickens the atoms (sparsity), and LFO 3 carries the window of the scale filter round the cycle.
1093 A2B1 · Clang CowbellA2 + B1A cowbell: two clanking partials, a hard strike and a short metallic ring; the scale cutoff breathes, LFO 2 widens and narrows the filtered window, LFO 3 sweeps the cutoff of the low-pass filter, and a 24 dB OTA low-pass (1512 Hz) that opens on every note.
1094 A1B1 · Round Rise DrumA1 + B1A tom: a round sine with a falling pitch and mirror atoms for the skin; LFO 2 changes how far each note's envelope opens the scale filter, and LFO 3 swells the resonance.
1095 A1A2 · Blip LaserA1 + A2An electronic zap: a sine that dives through the octaves with a hiss on top; LFO 2 carries the window of the scale filter round the cycle, and LFO 3 changes how far each note's envelope opens the scale filter.
1096 A1B1 · Thunder Orbit BoomA1 + B1A taiko: a very low sine that falls and a big skin that rings in a large room; LFO 2 carries the window of the scale filter round the cycle, and LFO 3 sweeps the scale filter.
1097 A1A2 · Blip Lung TomA1 + A2An electronic zap: a sine that dives through the octaves with a hiss on top; the scale cutoff breathes, LFO 2 carries the window of the scale filter round the cycle, LFO 3 pulses the resonance of the low-pass filter, and a 24 dB ladder low-pass (5534 Hz) that opens on every note with a singing resonance.
1098 A2B1 · Cowbell & SteelA2 + B1A cowbell: two clanking partials, a hard strike and a short metallic ring; the wavelet morphs towards Daubechies 20, and LFO 3 carries the window of the scale filter round the cycle.
1099 A2B1 · Bongo AtomsA2 + B1A hand drum: a round open tone with a slap, the pitch settling down as it rings; each note's envelope pushes the wavelet towards Daubechies 2, by an amount the LFO swings, the sparsity swings between a rich tone and a few wavelet atoms, and LFO 3 sweeps the scale filter.
1100 A1A2 · Morph ThumpA1 + A2A kick: a sine that falls from high to low in a blink, with a click on top; the wavelet morphs towards Haar, LFO 3 pulls the source wave between narrow and wide, LFO 2 pulses the resonance of the low-pass filter, and a 24 dB OTA low-pass (2213 Hz) that opens on every note with some bite.
189 A1A2 · KickA1 + A2A deep kick: a sine that falls from high to low in a blink, a short click on top

192 A1A2 · Open HatA1 + A2An open hi-hat: the same shimmer, left to ring for most of a second

12Evaluate sounds

EVALUATE SOUNDS (header, web app and plug-in) is a listening test of the 1160 new presets: it plays a piece of music with each one, asks how much you like it, and writes your answers — and the changes you make to the sounds you like — in text files you can send back. The sounds come in sets of 40: every set has its own file.

  1. Click EVALUATE SOUNDS, then Start at…: a small window asks the number of the first sound to rate (it offers the first one not rated yet; a later session also offers Continue at …). The list is the factory order: the 852 presets of beta 0.3 first (501–1400), then those of beta 0.2 (175), then those of beta 0.1 (133). The window plays 3.5 seconds of Beethoven's Appassionata (the busy passage at 0:13 of the demo) and then a C minor chord (two seconds: a sustained sound can be judged too, not only quick notes) with that preset — polyphonic, at its normal octave, with short echoes — and shows its name, category and one-line description.
  2. When the passage ends, give 1 to 10 stars: click a star (in the web app also the keys 1–9, 0 = 10). The answer is saved at once and the next preset plays. You can also play the preset on your MIDI keyboard while the window waits. ▶ Replay, ◀ Previous, Skip and Stop are always there, and Skip to vote ends the music at once and asks for the stars; Stop (or the lit EVALUATE button) gives back the sound you were playing.
  3. More than 5 stars asks whether to modify the preset. If yes, the preset loads exactly as designed, the window shrinks to a bar over the keyboard, and you change the panel with the mouse and play it on your keyboard. ▶ Play the passage plays it again. Keep initial settings forgets your changes; Record preset change writes the parameters that differ from the preset in the file.
  4. Sets of 40 sounds. The first set starts at the sound you chose, the next ones every 40 sounds, and every set has its own file, named after its first and last sound: sound 501 to sound 540.txt. Send back the files of the sets you rated.
  5. Stars in the preset browser (beta 0.3.1): the browser shows five stars (half stars: 1 to 10) at the right of every sound, in normal playing too; click to rate, click the same rating to clear it. The ratings are saved in the presets file and EXPORT carries them, whenever you like.
  6. Stop whenever you like: the files are rewritten after every answer, and the next session continues where you stopped (it reads the ratings file again).

The ratings file

  • Plug-in: Documents\Okto MultiWave Synth\okto-multiwave-ratings.txt (the local application data folder when the Documents folder is missing) holds every rating; the file of each set (sound xxx to sound yyy.txt) is written next to it after every answer. Change file… chooses another ratings file (an existing ratings file continues where it stopped; the choice is remembered; the set files go next to it), Show file opens its folder, Save as… saves a copy of the ratings so far under a name you choose (while a set is being rated, a copy of that set), Clear all starts over.
  • Web app: the ratings are kept in the browser; in Edge or Chrome you can choose a ratings file at the start (it is rewritten after every answer), and the file of a set is downloaded when the set is finished (every sound rated) and you move on, stop or finish. In other browsers Save as… downloads a copy (it also works in Edge and Chrome, with the name you choose).
  • The text is the same in both, for the whole list and for a set: a header, NUMBER | STARS | CHANGED | PRESET for every preset, a ranking, and PRESET CHANGES as parameter key: value in the preset -> new value (name: shown value -> new shown value).

13The plug-in

The plug-in runs the same sound engine with the same panel. It is a single self-contained file inside an OktoMultiWaveSynth.vst3 bundle folder, which also carries these manuals; nothing else needs to be installed. The same file is a VST3 and a VST2 plug-in (a copy named OktoMultiWaveSynth.dll sits next to it for VST2 hosts). Windows 10/11, 64-bit.

Installation

  1. Download the zip from the release page and unzip it.
  2. Copy the whole OktoMultiWaveSynth.vst3 folder into C:\Program Files\Common Files\VST3 (vst\install.bat does it and asks for administrator rights), or into the VST2 plug-in folder you use.
  3. In Live: Preferences → Plug-ins, switch on Use VST3 Plug-in System Folders (or the VST2 custom folder), Rescan. If both are on and see the folder, Live lists the plug-in twice; use either.

It installs next to the Wavelet Synthesizer and the WaveChord Synth: its own name, class IDs, VST2 ID and presets file.

What is specific to the plug-in

  • No welcome window, no DEMO or DOWNLOAD VST button, no status bar. Audio runs as soon as the plug-in is loaded. The window-size button (70–150 %) is next to the logo.
  • Tempo sync: the SYNC buttons of the delay and the two LFOs (see Tempo sync).
  • Presets: ◀ ▶ step through the factory presets (those you deleted are skipped); a click on the name opens the preset browser (FACTORY and USER BANK tabs; HIDE ▸ folds it away), a right-click the classic menu. In the browser: ▲ ▼ move a sound within its list, a sound dragged onto a category moves there (onto TOP Sounds it joins them), the arrow keys choose, and the buttons TO BANK…, RESTORE, DELETE, EXPORT…, IMPORT… manage your sounds.
  • SAVE PRESET (left of MANUAL) overwrites the preset playing: a factory preset is adjusted in place, optionally under a new name (RESTORE brings the original back); a sound of the user bank is replaced.
  • The presets file: everything above lives in one readable text file, OKTOMULTIWAVE presets.txt, kept next to the .vst3 folder (the folder must allow writing: C:\Program Files\Common Files\VST3 needs administrator rights, so install into a folder you own, or run Live as administrator once). It is written atomically after every edit and read again when another instance changes it. A file longer than 16 MB is never written over.
  • Automation: every sound parameter and the chord harp's six settings can be automated; the oscillator tabs (OSC) are a panel choice, not part of a sound.
  • EVALUATE SOUNDS works in the plug-in too, with the host's audio engine running (see Evaluate sounds). While a session runs the preset browser, SAVE PRESET and the arrow keys are locked.

14Troubleshooting

No sound at all in the web app

Browsers only allow sound after a click: click Play the synth in the welcome window (or a key of the keyboard). The status bar must say Audio on. Only one window or tab plays at a time: another tab of the app may have taken the audio.

I click an oscillator tab and the sound does not change

By design: the tabs only choose the panel shown. The sound is set by the three levels in the OSC MIX card. A tab shows the cards of an oscillator whose level may be 0.

A knob I turn has no effect

Check the level of the oscillator whose panel you are editing (OSC MIX) — an oscillator at 0 is silent — and whether the control is greyed out (WIDTH, SEED, the sub oscillator). With the filter wide open, the cutoff does nothing.

The filter does nothing on a SQUARE with Haar

A square analysed with Haar is a single atom (see Manual A): choose a smoother wavelet.

A preset sounds quieter or louder than another

The new presets are matched to the TOP Sounds, but very short sounds (hats, clicks) are measured over 50 ms. Use the master VOLUME, or the level knobs.

The plug-in does not appear in Live

Copy the whole bundle folder (not only the file inside), check Use VST3 Plug-in System Folders and press Rescan; the folder must be 64-bit Windows.

SAVE PRESET says it cannot write

The presets file sits next to the .vst3 folder; that folder must allow writing (see What is specific to the plug-in).

EVALUATE SOUNDS says the passage did not play

The host's audio engine must be running: in Live, turn the audio engine on and arm or enable the track. The window waits a few seconds, then continues so that you can still rate the preset.

Where is my ratings file?

Plug-in: Documents\Okto MultiWave Synth\okto-multiwave-ratings.txt and, next to it, one sound xxx to sound yyy.txt per set (use Show file). Web app: the file you chose at the start, or the downloads (one file per finished set).

The CPU is high

Cost = voices × unison copies × oscillators that are on, and the voice FILTER (about the cost of one more oscillator) when it is not open. Lower UNISON or VOICES, turn off oscillators you do not hear, open the FILTER, avoid an LFO on PHASE with many voices.

15More, and a glossary

Okto MultiWave Synth is built on the Wavelet Synthesizer. Its manuals explain the ideas behind each oscillator kind in depth — partials versus atoms, how many atoms a wave needs, source phase, the scale envelope, the full recipes — for the original panel, where the two engines shared one filter and one window:

  • Wavelet Synthesizer — Manual A (Wavelet Subtractive)
  • Wavelet Synthesizer — Manual B (Wavelet Additive)

Everything in them about a source wave, an atom pattern, the scale filter or the position window applies to each oscillator of Okto MultiWave Synth (each has its own); what they say about the A/B MIX knob is replaced by the three levels of OSC MIX.

Glossary

TermMeaning
AtomOne small wiggle of a wavelet transform; all atoms of a wavelet have the same shape, in sizes an octave apart.
BandAll atoms of one size: band b has 2b−1 atoms, about one octave of harmonics. A cycle has 11 bands.
ScaleThe size of an atom, i.e. the octave: a "scale filter" acts on bands, not on frequencies.
WaveletThe shape of the atoms: Haar, Daubechies 2/4/8/20, Symlet 8, Coiflet 3.
SparsityKeeping only the largest atoms of a cycle (like a lossy codec).
Source phaseMoving the source wave inside the cycle before the analysis: other atoms, other timbre.
Position windowA bump inside the cycle that confines the filter (A) or the detail (B) to one part of it.
SuperModulationAn LFO moving the wavelet-domain controls — scale cutoff and resonance, sparsity, window, source phase and width, wavelet morph, additive scales — while a note sounds; also the first category of presets.
FILTERThe voice filter: a four-pole resonant low-pass (LADDER or OTA character) with its own ADSR, before the AMP ENVELOPE. It is open by default.
Scale filterThe filter of each oscillator, which gives each wavelet band its own gain (it cuts scales, not frequencies).
Quarter tone, commaHalf of a semitone (50 cents), used by the Arabic tuning; the Turkish tuning divides the octave into 53 commas of 22.6 cents.
Own envelopeAn oscillator's private ADSR; it multiplies the AMP ENVELOPE.
Pitch envelopeA glide of an oscillator from some semitones away to the note.
OSC MIXThe three oscillator levels.
TOP SoundsOktoPierre's 22 favourite presets, first in the list.
